We inductively compute the scalar product and obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{l+1}:

\displaystyle=\widetilde{H}\left(\left[\cup_{i=l+1}^{r+1}\rho_{vB^{(i)},1,a_{i-1}}\right]\cup\left[\cup_{i=l+1}^{r}\rho_{vW^{(i)},2,a_{i}}\right]\cup\rho_{vY^{(l)}),1,a_{l}}\right)

\displaystyle H\left(\rho_{Y^{(l)},1,a_{l}};\left[\cup_{i=l+1}^{r}\rho_{A^{(i)},1,a_{i}}\right]\right)\cdot\frac{H(\rho_{Y^{(l)},1,a_{l}},\cup_{i=l+1}^{r}\rho_{W^{(i)},1,a_{i}})}{H(\rho_{Y^{(l)},1,a_{l}},\cup_{i=l+1}^{r}\rho_{t^{2\sigma(a_{i})-1}W^{(i)},a_{i}})}\cdot C_{1};

where

\displaystyle C_{1}

\displaystyle=\left[\prod_{j=l+1}^{r}\prod_{i=j+1}^{r}H\left(\rho_{B^{(j)},1,a_{j-1}};\rho_{A^{(i)},1,a_{i}}\right)\right]\cdot\left[\prod_{j=l+1}^{r}\prod_{i=j+1}^{r}\frac{H(\rho_{B^{(j)},1,a_{j-1}},\rho_{W^{(i)},1,a_{i}})}{H(\rho_{B^{(j)},1,a_{j-1}},\rho_{t^{2\sigma(a_{i})-1}W^{(i)},1,a_{i}})}\right]

\displaystyle\left[\prod_{j=l+1}^{r}\prod_{i=j+1}^{r}\frac{H(\rho_{A^{(i)},1,a_{i}},\rho_{t^{1-2\sigma(a_{j})}(W^{(j)})^{-1},1,a_{j}})}{H(\rho_{A^{(i)},1,a_{i}},\rho_{(W^{(j)})^{-1},1,a_{j}})}\right]\cdot\left[\prod_{j=l+1}^{r}\prod_{i=j+1}^{r}\frac{H(\rho_{W^{(i)},1,a_{i}},\rho_{t^{1-2\sigma(a_{j})}(W^{(j)})^{-1},1,a_{j}})}{H(\rho_{W^{(i)},1,a_{i}},\rho_{(W^{(j)})^{-1},1,a_{j}})}\right]

\displaystyle\left[\prod_{j=l+1}^{r}\prod_{i=j+1}^{r}\frac{H(\rho_{t^{2\sigma(a_{i})-1}W^{(i)},1,a_{i}},\rho_{(W^{(j)})^{-1},1,a_{j}})}{H(\rho_{t^{2\sigma(a_{i})-1}W^{(i)},1,a_{i}},\rho_{t^{1-2\sigma(a_{j})}(W^{(j)})^{-1},1,a_{j}})}\right]

Then we have

\displaystyle\langle F_{l},\mathcal{F}_{l+1}\rangle_{Y^{(l)}}=\frac{C_{1}}{C_{2}}\langle\widetilde{H}(\rho_{uA^{(l)},1,a_{l}}\cup\rho_{uY^{(l)},1,a_{l}},\widetilde{H}\left(\left[\cup_{i=l+1}^{r+1}\rho_{vB^{(i)},1,a_{i-1}}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.\cup\left[\cup_{i=l+1}^{r}\rho_{vW^{(i)},t,2,a_{i}}\right]\cup\left[\cup_{i=l+1}^{r}\rho_{\frac{1}{v}A^{(i)},1,a_{i}}\right]\cup\left[\cup_{i=l+1}^{r}(-1)\rho_{\frac{1}{v}[W^{(i)}]^{-1},t^{-1},2,a_{i}^{\prime}}\right]\cup\rho_{vY^{(l)}),1,a_{l}}\right)\rangle_{Y^{(l)}}

where

\displaystyle C_{2}:

\displaystyle=\widetilde{H}\left(\left[\cup_{i=l+1}^{r}\rho_{\frac{1}{v}A^{(i)},1,a_{i}}\right]\cup\left[\cup_{i=l+1}^{r}(-1)*\rho_{\frac{1}{v}[W^{(i)}]^{-1},t^{-1},2,a_{i}}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\times H(\left[\cup_{i=l+1}^{r}\rho_{\frac{1}{v}A^{(i)},1,a_{i}}\right]\cup\left[\cup_{i=l+1}^{r}(-1)*\rho_{\frac{1}{v}[W^{(i)}]^{-1},t^{-1},2,a_{i}};\left[\cup_{i=l+1}^{r+1}\rho_{vB^{(i)},1,a_{i-1}}\right]\cup\left[\cup_{i=l+1}^{r}\rho_{vW^{(i)},t,2,a_{i}}\right]\right];)

By Lemma
3.12
, we obtain

\displaystyle\langle F_{l},\mathcal{F}_{l+1}\rangle_{Y^{(l)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{1}}{C_{2}}\cdot\prod_{n\geq 1}\frac{\widetilde{H}([u^{n-1}v^{n}]\rho_{2})\widetilde{H}([u^{n}v^{n-1}]\rho_{1})H(u^{2n}\rho_{1};v^{2n}\rho_{2})}{1-u^{n}v^{n}}

where

\displaystyle\rho_{1}

\displaystyle=\rho_{A^{(l)},1,a_{l}};

\displaystyle\rho_{2}

\displaystyle=\left[\cup_{i=l+1}^{r+1}\rho_{B^{(i)},1,a_{i-1}}\right]\cup\left[\cup_{i=l+1}^{r}\rho_{W^{(i)},t,2,a_{i}}\right]\cup\left[\cup_{i=l+1}^{r}\rho_{\frac{1}{v^{2}}A^{(i)},1,a_{i}}\right]\cup\left[\cup_{i=l+1}^{r}(-1)*\rho_{\frac{1}{v^{2}}[W^{(i)}]^{-1},t^{-1},2,a_{i}}\right]

After the cancellation of the partition function in both the numerator and the denominator, the lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 3.9
.

Let
\{d_{k}\}_{k}
,
\{s_{k}\}_{k}
be sequences of elements of the graded algebra
\mathcal{A}
and let
\{u_{k}\}_{k}
be a sequence of elements of the graded algebra
\mathcal{B}
. Assume
\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\mathrm{ldeg}(d_{k})=\infty
,

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\mathrm{ldeg}(s_{k})=\infty

and

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\mathrm{ldeg}(u_{k})=\infty
; where
\mathrm{ldeg}
is defined as in (
A.1
). For each non-negative integer
k
, let
p_{k}
be the power sum as defined in (
A.2
). Then

\displaystyle\left\langle\exp\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{d_{k}p_{k}(Y)+s_{k}[p_{k}(Y)]^{2}}{k}\right),\exp\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{u_{k}p_{k}(Y)}{k}\right)\right\rangle_{Y;q=t}=\exp\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{d_{k}u_{k}+s_{k}u_{k}^{2}}{k}\right)\right)

where
a_{k}
,
b_{k}
are independent of the variables in
Y
.

Proof.

When
s_{k}=0
, the lemma follows from Proposition 2.3 of
[
6
]
. For general
s_{k}
, the lemma follows from the definition of the Macdonald scalar product (
A.5
). More precisely, let

\displaystyle c_{(k_{1},l_{1}),(k_{2},l_{2}),\ldots,(k_{m},l_{m})}[p_{k_{1}}(Y)]^{l_{1}}[p_{k_{2}}(Y)]^{l_{2}}\cdots[p_{k_{m}}(Y)]^{l_{m}}:=f(Y)

be a monomial in the expansion of

\displaystyle\exp\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{d_{k}p_{k}(Y)+s_{k}[p_{k}(Y)]^{2}}{k}\right);

such that
k_{1}<k_{2}<\ldots<k_{m}
. Note that

(3.7)

\displaystyle R(y):=\exp\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{u_{k}p_{k}(Y)}{k}\right)\right)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{u_{k}p_{k}(Y)}{k}\right)\right)^{n}

Let
L:=l_{1}+\ldots+l_{m}
. The monomial

[p_{k_{1}}(Y)]^{l_{1}}[p_{k_{2}}(Y)]^{l_{2}}\cdots[p_{k_{m}}(Y)]^{l_{m}}
has coefficient

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L!}\frac{L!}{(l_{1})!(l_{2})!\cdot\ldots\cdot(l_{m})!}\frac{u_{k_{1}}^{l_{1}}u_{k_{2}}^{l_{2}}\cdot\ldots\cdot u_{k_{m}}^{l_{m}}}{k_{1}^{l_{1}}k_{2}^{l_{2}}\cdot\ldots\cdot k_{m}^{l_{m}}}:=\frac{g(Y)}{[p_{k_{1}}(Y)]^{l_{1}}[p_{k_{2}}(Y)]^{l_{2}}\cdots[p_{k_{m}}(Y)]^{l_{m}}}

Then by (
A.5
)

\displaystyle\langle f(Y),R(y)\rangle_{Y;q=t}=\langle f(Y),g(Y)\rangle_{Y;q=t}=c_{(k_{1},l_{1}),(k_{2},l_{2}),\ldots,(k_{m},l_{m})}u_{k_{1}}^{l_{1}}u_{k_{2}}^{l_{2}}\cdot\ldots\cdot u_{k_{m}}^{l_{m}}

which can be obtained from
f(Y)
by replacing each
p_{k_{i}}(Y)
by
u_{k_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq m
.
∎

Lemma 3.10
.

Let
\rho_{1},\rho_{2},\rho_{3}
be three specializations such that
\rho_{1},\rho_{3}
are independent of
Y
.

(1)

If

\displaystyle\rho_{2}(p_{k})=p_{k}(Y),\ \forall k\geq 1,

Then

\displaystyle\left\langle\widetilde{H}(\rho_{1}\cup\rho_{2}),\exp\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\rho_{3}(p_{k})p_{k}(Y)}{k}\right)\right\rangle_{Y,q=t}=\widetilde{H}(\rho_{1}\cup\rho_{3})

(2)

If

\displaystyle\rho_{2}(p_{k})=(-1)^{k+1}p_{k}(Y),\ \forall k\geq 1,

Then

\displaystyle\left\langle\widetilde{H}(\rho_{1}\cup\rho_{2}),\exp\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{k+1}\rho_{3}(p_{k})p_{k}(Y)}{k}\right)\right\rangle_{Y,q=t}=\widetilde{H}(\rho_{1}\cup\rho_{3})

Proof.

The lemma follows from the Definition
A.4
of
\widetilde{H}
and Lemma
3.9
.
∎

Lemma 3.11
.

Let
\rho
be a specialization. Then

\displaystyle\sum_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{Y}}}s_{\lambda/\mu}(\rho)u^{|\lambda|}=\widetilde{H}(u\rho)\sum_{\xi\in{\mathbb{Y}}}s_{\mu/\xi}(u^{2}\rho)u^{|\xi|}

Proof.

From Example I.5 23 (a)(3) in
[
33
]
, we see that

(3.8)

\displaystyle\sum_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{Y}}}s_{\lambda/\mu}(X)=\frac{1}{\prod_{x_{i}\in X}(1-x_{i})\prod_{x_{i},x_{j}\in X,i<j}(1-x_{i}x_{j})}\sum_{\xi\in{\mathbb{Y}}}s_{\mu/\xi}(X)

where
X
is a countable set of variables. If we replace each
p_{k}(X)
by
u^{k}\rho(p_{k})
in (
3.8
), we obtain

(3.9)

\displaystyle\sum_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{Y}}}s_{\lambda/\mu}(u\rho)=\widetilde{H}(u\rho)\sum_{\xi\in{\mathbb{Y}}}s_{\mu/\xi}(u\rho)

Multiplying both the LHS and RHS by
u^{|\mu|}
gives us the result.
∎

Lemma 3.12
.

Let
\rho_{1},\rho_{2}
be specializations. Let
Y
be a countable set of variables. Let
\rho
be the specialization given by

\displaystyle\rho(p_{k})=p_{k}(Y)

Let
u,v\in(0,1)
. Then

\displaystyle\langle\widetilde{H}(u[\rho_{1}\cup\rho]),\widetilde{H}(v[\rho_{2}\cup\rho])\rangle_{Y;q=t}=\prod_{n\geq 1}\frac{\widetilde{H}([u^{n-1}v^{n}]\rho_{2})\widetilde{H}([u^{n}v^{n-1}]\rho_{1})H([u^{2n}]\rho_{1};[v^{2n}]\rho_{2})}{1-u^{n}v^{n}}

Proof.

\displaystyle\langle\widetilde{H}(u[\rho_{1}\cup\rho]),\widetilde{H}(v[\rho_{2}\cup\rho])\rangle_{Y;q=t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda,\mu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}\langle u^{|\lambda|}s_{\lambda}([\rho_{1}\cup\rho]),v^{|\mu|}s_{\mu}([\rho_{2}\cup\rho])\rangle_{Y,q=t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda,\mu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}u^{|\lambda|}v^{|\mu|}\sum_{\nu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}s_{\lambda/\nu}(\rho_{1})s_{\mu/\nu}(\rho_{2})

By Lemma
3.11
, we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\widetilde{H}(u[\rho_{1}\cup\rho]),\widetilde{H}(v[\rho_{2}\cup\rho])\rangle_{Y;q=t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\nu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}\left[\sum_{\lambda,\in{\mathbb{Y}}}u^{|\lambda|}s_{\lambda/\nu}(\rho_{1})\right]\left[\sum_{\mu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}v^{|\mu|}s_{\mu/\nu}(\rho_{2})\right]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{H}(u\rho_{1})\sum_{\nu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}\left[\sum_{\xi\in{\mathbb{Y}}}u^{|\xi|}s_{\nu/\xi}(u^{2}\rho_{1})\right]\left[\sum_{\mu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}v^{|\mu|}s_{\mu/\nu}(\rho_{2})\right]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{H}(u\rho_{1})\sum_{\xi,\mu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}u^{|\xi|}v^{|\mu|}\sum_{\nu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}s_{\nu/\xi}(u^{2}\rho_{1})s_{\mu/\nu}(\rho_{2})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{H}(u\rho_{1})\sum_{\xi,\mu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}u^{|\xi|}v^{|\mu|}\sum_{\nu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}s_{\nu/\xi}(\rho_{2})s_{\mu/\nu}(u^{2}\rho_{1})

Again by Lemma
3.11
, we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\widetilde{H}(u[\rho_{1}\cup\rho]),\widetilde{H}(v[\rho_{2}\cup\rho])\rangle_{Y;q=t}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{H}(u\rho_{1})\sum_{\nu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}\left[\sum_{\xi\in{\mathbb{Y}}}u^{|\xi|}s_{\nu/\xi}(\rho_{2})\right]\left[\sum_{\mu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}s_{\mu/\nu}(u\rho_{1})v^{|\mu|}\right]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{H}(u\rho_{1})\widetilde{H}([u^{2}v]\rho_{1})\sum_{\nu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}\left[\sum_{\xi\in{\mathbb{Y}}}u^{|\xi|}s_{\nu/\xi}(\rho_{2})\right]\left[\sum_{\tau\in{\mathbb{Y}}}s_{\nu/\tau}([u^{2}v^{2}]\rho_{1})v^{|\tau|}\right]

From Example I.5.26 (1), we obtain the following formula

\displaystyle\sum_{\nu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}s_{\nu/\xi}(\rho_{2})s_{\nu/\tau}([u^{2}v^{2}]\rho_{1})=H([u^{2}v^{2}]\rho_{1};\rho_{2})\sum_{\nu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}s_{\xi/\nu}([u^{2}v^{2}]\rho_{1})s_{\tau/\nu}(\rho_{2})

Then we have

\displaystyle\langle\widetilde{H}(u[\rho_{1}\cup\rho]),\widetilde{H}(v[\rho_{2}\cup\rho])\rangle_{Y;q=t}=\widetilde{H}(u\rho_{1})\widetilde{H}([u^{2}v]\rho_{1})\sum_{\xi,\tau\in{\mathbb{Y}}}u^{|\xi|}v^{|\tau|}\sum_{\nu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}s_{\nu/\xi}(\rho_{2})s_{\nu/\tau}([u^{2}v^{2}]\rho_{1})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{H}(u\rho_{1})\widetilde{H}([u^{2}v]\rho_{1})H([u^{2}v^{2}]\rho_{1};\rho_{2})\sum_{\nu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}\sum_{\xi\in{\mathbb{Y}}}u^{|\xi|}s_{\xi/\nu}([u^{2}v^{2}]\rho_{1})\sum_{\tau\in{\mathbb{Y}}}v^{|\tau|}s_{\tau/\nu}(\rho_{2})

Again by Lemma
3.11
, we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\widetilde{H}(u[\rho_{1}\cup\rho]),\widetilde{H}(v[\rho_{2}\cup\rho])\rangle_{Y;q=t}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{H}(u\rho_{1})\widetilde{H}([u^{2}v]\rho_{1})H([u^{2}v^{2}]\rho_{1};\rho_{2})\widetilde{H}(v\rho_{2})\sum_{\nu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}\sum_{\xi\in{\mathbb{Y}}}u^{|\xi|}s_{\xi/\nu}([u^{2}v^{2}]\rho_{1})\sum_{\tau\in{\mathbb{Y}}}v^{|\tau|}s_{\nu/\tau}(v^{2}\rho_{2})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{H}(u\rho_{1})\widetilde{H}([u^{2}v]\rho_{1})H([u^{2}v^{2}]\rho_{1};\rho_{2})\widetilde{H}(v\rho_{2})\sum_{\xi,\tau\in{\mathbb{Y}}}\sum_{\nu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}u^{|\xi|}s_{\xi/\nu}([u^{2}v^{2}]\rho_{2})v^{|\tau|}s_{\nu/\tau}(v^{2}\rho_{1})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{H}(u\rho_{1})\widetilde{H}([u^{2}v]\rho_{1})H([u^{2}v^{2}]\rho_{1};\rho_{2})\widetilde{H}(v\rho_{2})\sum_{\nu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}\sum_{\xi\in{\mathbb{Y}}}u^{|\xi|}s_{\xi/\nu}(v^{2}\rho_{1})\sum_{\tau\in{\mathbb{Y}}}v^{|\tau|}s_{\nu/\tau}([u^{2}v^{2}]\rho_{2})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{H}(u\rho_{1})\widetilde{H}([u^{2}v]\rho_{1})H([u^{2}v^{2}]\rho_{1};\rho_{2})\widetilde{H}(v\rho_{2})\widetilde{H}([uv^{2}]\rho_{1})

\displaystyle\times\left[\sum_{\nu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}\left[\sum_{\xi\in{\mathbb{Y}}}u^{|\xi|}s_{\nu/\xi}([u^{2}v^{2}]\rho_{1})\right]\left[\sum_{\tau\in{\mathbb{Y}}}v^{|\tau|}s_{\nu/\tau}([u^{2}v^{2}]\rho_{2})\right]\right]

We consider
u^{2}v^{2}\rho_{1}=\rho_{1}^{(1)}
,
u^{2}v^{2}\rho_{2}=\rho_{2}^{(1)}
, and iterate the above process, we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\widetilde{H}(u[\rho_{1}\cup\rho]),\widetilde{H}(v[\rho_{2}\cup\rho])\rangle_{Y;q=t}

\displaystyle=\left[\prod_{i=1}^{2n}\widetilde{H}([u^{i}v^{i-1}]\rho_{1})\widetilde{H}([v^{i}u^{i-1}]\rho_{2})\right]\left[\prod_{j=1}^{n}H([u^{2j}v^{2j}]\rho_{1};\rho_{2})\right]

\displaystyle\times\left(\sum_{\nu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}\sum_{\xi\in{\mathbb{Y}}}u^{|\xi|}s_{\nu/\xi}([u^{2n}v^{2n}]\rho_{1})\sum_{\tau\in{\mathbb{Y}}}v^{|\tau|}s_{\nu/\tau}([u^{2n}v^{2n}]\rho_{2})\right)

Note that when
u,v\in(0,1)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{\nu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}\sum_{\xi\in{\mathbb{Y}}}u^{|\xi|}s_{\nu/\xi}([u^{2n}v^{2n}]\rho_{1})\sum_{\tau\in{\mathbb{Y}}}v^{|\tau|}s_{\nu/\tau}([u^{2n}v^{2n}]\rho_{2})=\sum_{\nu\in{\mathbb{Y}}}u^{|\nu|}v^{|\nu|}=\prod_{n\geq 1}\frac{1}{1-u^{n}v^{n}}.

Then the lemma follows.
∎

4.
Asymptotics

In Section
4
, we study the asymptotics of the moments of the random height functions and prove its Gaussian fluctuation in the scaling limit.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
i_{1}\leq i_{2}\leq\ldots\leq i_{m}\in[l+1..r]
, and let
l_{1},\ldots,l_{m}>0
be integers. Let
I:=\{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{m}\}
. Then

\displaystyle\left.{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{Pr}}\left[\prod_{s\in[m]}\gamma_{l_{s}}(\lambda^{(i_{s})};q,t)\right]\right|_{q=t}

\displaystyle=\oint\ldots\oint\prod_{s\in[m]}D(W^{(i_{s})};\omega(t,t,a_{i_{s}}))\times\prod_{s\in[m];j\in[i_{s}..r],b_{j}=-}H(\rho_{W^{(i_{s})},t,2,a_{i_{s}}};\rho_{\{x_{j}\},1,a_{j}})

\displaystyle\times\prod_{s\in[m];j\in[l..i_{s}-1],b_{j}=+}H((-1)*\rho_{[W^{(i_{s})}]^{-1},t^{-1},2,a_{i_{s}}};\rho_{\{x_{j}\},1,a_{j}})\times\prod_{u<v;u,v\in[m]}T_{a_{i_{u}},a_{i_{v}}}(W^{(i)},W^{(j)})

\displaystyle\times\prod_{k\geq 1}\widetilde{H}([u^{k-1}v^{k}]\rho_{W_{I}})\widetilde{H}([u^{k}v^{k-1}]\rho_{W^{\circ}_{I}})H(u^{2k}\rho_{A};v^{2k}\rho_{W_{I}})H(u^{2k}\rho_{A};v^{2k-2}\rho_{W^{\circ}_{I}}))

\displaystyle\times\prod_{k\geq 1}H(u^{2k}\rho_{B};v^{2k}\rho_{W^{\circ}_{I}})H(u^{2k-2}\rho_{B};v^{2k}\rho_{W_{I}}))H(u^{2k}\rho_{W_{I}};v^{2k}\rho_{W^{\circ}_{I}});

where the expectation is with respect to the probability measure (
2.7
);
A^{(i)}
,
B^{(i)}
are given as in Lemma
3.5
;
|W^{i_{s}}|=|l_{i_{s}}|
, and

\displaystyle\rho_{W_{I}}

\displaystyle=\cup_{s\in[m]}\rho_{W^{(i_{s})},t,2,a_{i_{s}}};\qquad\rho_{W^{\circ}_{I}}=\cup_{s\in[m]}(-1)*\rho_{[W^{(i_{s})]^{-1}},t^{-1},2,a_{i_{s}}}.

Proof.

The corollary follows from Lemmas
3.5
and
3.8
. The major difference between this lemma and Lemma
4.1
lies in

(1)

The Macdonald process is specialized with
A^{(i)}
,
B^{(i)}
given as in Lemma
3.5
.

(2)

The index set
I
may not include all the numbers in
[l+1..r]
; in this case simply for
j\in[l+1..r]\setminus I
, simply consider
\gamma_{0}(\lambda^{j};q,t)=1
;

(3)

An integer
i\in[l+1..r]
may repeat multiple times in
\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{m}\}
, in this case the lemma follows from similar arguments as in Corollary 3.11 in
[
6
]
.

∎

Lemma 4.2
.

Under the same conditions as Lemma
4.1
and assuming the index set
I
is a subset of
\mathcal{L}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

{}_{\mathrm{Pr}}\left[\prod_{s\in[m]}\gamma_{l_{s}}(\lambda^{(M,i_{s})};t,t)\right]=\oint\ldots\oint\prod_{s\in[m]}\left[D(W^{(i_{s})};t,t)\right.

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[i_{s}..r]\\
b_{j}=-,a_{j}=L\end{subarray}}G_{1,>}(W^{(i_{s})},x_{j},t)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[i_{s}..r]\\
b_{j}=-,a_{j}=R\end{subarray}}G_{0,>}(W^{(i_{s})},x_{j},t)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[l..i_{s}-1]\\
b_{j}=+,a_{i}=L\end{subarray}}G_{1,<}(W^{(i_{s})},x_{j},t)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[l..i_{s}-1]\\
b_{j}=+,a_{i}=R\end{subarray}}G_{0,<}(W^{(i_{s})},x_{j},t)

\displaystyle\left(\prod_{k\geq 1}\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=-,a_{i}=L}G_{1,>}(W^{(i_{s})},u^{2k}v^{2k}x_{j},t)G_{1,<}(W^{(i_{s})},u^{2k}v^{2k-2}x_{j},t)\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=-,a_{i}=R}G_{0,>}(W^{(i_{s})},u^{2k}v^{2k}x_{j},t)G_{0,<}(W^{(i_{s})},u^{2k}v^{2k-2}x_{j},t)\right]

\displaystyle\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=+,a_{i}=L}G_{1,<}(W^{(i_{s})},u^{2k}v^{2k}x_{j},t)G_{1,>}(W^{(i_{s})},u^{2k-2}v^{2k}x_{j},t)\right]

\displaystyle\left.\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=+,a_{i}=R}G_{0,<}(W^{(i_{s})},u^{2k}v^{2k}x_{j},t)G_{0,>}(W^{(i_{s})},u^{2k-2}v^{2k}x_{j},t)\right]\right)

\displaystyle\prod_{1\leq s<j\leq m}T_{L,L}(W^{(i_{s})},W^{(i_{j})})\prod_{k\geq 1}\left(\widetilde{H}([u^{k}v^{k-1}]\rho_{W_{I}}^{\circ})\widetilde{H}([u^{k-1}v^{k}]\rho_{W_{I}})\prod_{s\in[m]}\prod_{g\in[m]}T_{L,L}(W^{(i_{s})},u^{2k}v^{2k}W^{(i_{g})})\right),

where
|W^{(i)}|=l_{i}
, and the integral contours are given by
\{\mathcal{C}_{i,j}\}_{i\in I,s\in[l_{i}]}
satisfying the condition as described in Lemma
3.8
, where

(4.1)

\displaystyle G_{1,>}(W,x,t)

\displaystyle=\prod_{w_{s}\in W}\frac{w_{s}-x}{w_{s}-tx},\quad G_{1,<}(W,x,t)=\prod_{w_{s}\in W}\frac{t-w_{s}x}{t(1-w_{s}x)},

(4.2)

\displaystyle G_{0,>}(W,x,t)

\displaystyle=\prod_{w_{s}\in W}\frac{w_{s}+tx}{w_{s}+x},\quad G_{0,<}(W,x,t)=\prod_{w_{s}\in W}\frac{t(1+w_{s}x)}{t+w_{s}x},

(4.3)

\displaystyle T_{L,L}(Z,W):=\prod_{z_{i}\in Z}\prod_{w_{j}\in W}\frac{\left(z_{i}-w_{j}\right)^{2}}{\left(z_{i}-t^{-1}w_{j}\right)\left(z_{i}-tw_{j}\right)}.

(4.4)

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}\left([u^{k-1}v^{k}]\rho_{W_{I}}\right)=\prod_{s\in[m]}\left[\prod_{w\in W^{(i_{s})}}\frac{1-u^{2k-2}v^{2k}tw^{-2}}{(1-u^{k-1}v^{k}tw^{-1})(1+u^{k-1}v^{k}w^{-1})}\right]

\displaystyle\times\prod_{1\leq s<g\leq m}T_{L,L}(W^{(i_{s})},u^{2k-2}v^{2k}t[W^{(i_{g})}]^{-1})

\displaystyle\times\prod_{s\in[m]}\left[\prod_{w_{j},w_{i}\in W^{(i_{s})},j<i}\frac{(1-u^{2k-2}v^{2k}tw_{j}^{-1}w_{i}^{-1})^{2}}{(1-u^{2k-2}v^{2k}t^{2}w_{j}^{-1}w_{i}^{-1})(1-u^{2k-2}v^{2k}w_{j}^{-1}w_{i}^{-1})}\right]

(4.5)

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}\left([u^{k}v^{k-1}]\rho_{W_{I}}^{\circ}\right)=\prod_{s\in[m]}\left[\prod_{w\in W^{(i)}}\frac{1-u^{2k}v^{2k-2}t^{-1}w^{2}}{(1-u^{k}v^{k-1}w)(1+u^{k}v^{k-1}t^{-1}w)}\right]

\displaystyle\times\prod_{1\leq s<g\leq m}T_{L,L}([W^{(i_{s})}]^{-1},u^{2k}v^{2k-2}t^{-1}W^{(i_{g})})

\displaystyle\times\prod_{s\in[m]}\left[\prod_{w_{j},w_{i}\in W^{(i_{s})},j<i}\frac{(1-u^{2k}v^{2k-2}t^{-1}w_{j}w_{i})^{2}}{(1-u^{2k}v^{2k-2}t^{-2}w_{j}w_{i})(1-u^{2k}v^{2k-2}w_{j}w_{i})}\right]

Proof.

Explicit computations show that

\displaystyle\prod_{s\in[m];j\in[i_{s}..r],b_{j}=-}H(\rho_{W^{(i_{s})},t,2,a_{i_{s}}};\rho_{\{x_{j}\},1,a_{j}})\prod_{s\in[m];j\in[l..i_{s}-1],b_{j}=+}H((-1)*\rho_{[W^{(i_{s})}]^{-1},t^{-1},2,a_{i_{s}}};\rho_{\{x_{j}\},1,a_{j}})

\displaystyle=\prod_{s\in[m]}\left[\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[i_{s}..r],b_{j}=-,a_{j}=L\end{subarray}}G_{1,>}(W^{(i_{s})},x_{j},t)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[i_{s}..r],b_{j}=-,a_{j}=R\end{subarray}}G_{0,>}(W^{(i_{s})},x_{j},t)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[l..i_{s}-1],b_{j}=+,a_{i}=L\end{subarray}}G_{1,<}(W^{(i_{s})},x_{j},t)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[l..i_{s}-1],b_{j}=+,a_{i}=R\end{subarray}}G_{0,<}(W^{(i_{s})},x_{j},t)\right]

Note that

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}\left([u^{k-1}v^{k}]\rho_{W_{I}}\right)=\prod_{s\in[m]}\left[\widetilde{H}\left([u^{k-1}v^{k}t]\rho_{[W^{(i_{s})}]^{-1},1,L}\right)\widetilde{H}\left([u^{k-1}v^{k}]\{(-1)*\rho_{[W^{(i_{s})}]^{-1},1,L}\}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\times\left[\prod_{1\leq s<g\leq m}H\left([u^{k-1}v^{k}t]\rho_{[W^{(i_{s})}]^{-1},1,L};[u^{k-1}v^{k}t]\rho_{[W^{(i_{g})}]^{-1},1,L}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\times\left[\prod_{s,g\in[m]}H\left([u^{k-1}v^{k}t]\rho_{[W^{(i_{s})}]^{-1},1,L};[u^{k-1}v^{k}]\{(-1)*\rho_{[W^{(i_{g})}]^{-1},1,L}\}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\times\left[\prod_{1\leq s<g\leq m}H\left([u^{k-1}v^{k}]\{(-1)*\rho_{[W^{(i_{s})}]^{-1},1,L}\};[u^{k-1}v^{k}]\{(-1)*\rho_{[W^{(i_{g})}]^{-1},1,L}\}\right)\right]

We have

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}\left([u^{k-1}v^{k}t]\rho_{[W^{(i)}]^{-1},1,L}\right)

\displaystyle=\left[\prod_{w\in W^{(i)}}\frac{1}{(1-u^{k-1}v^{k}tw^{-1})}\right]\left[\prod_{w_{j},w_{s}\in W^{(i)},j<s}\frac{1}{(1-u^{2k-2}v^{2k}t^{2}w_{j}^{-1}w_{s}^{-1})}\right]

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}\left([u^{k-1}v^{k}]\{(-1)*\rho_{[W^{(i)}]^{-1},1,L}\}\right)=\left[\prod_{w\in W^{(i)}}\frac{1}{(1+u^{k-1}v^{k}w^{-1})}\right]\left[\prod_{w_{j},w_{s}\in W^{(i)},j<s}\frac{1}{(1-u^{2k-2}v^{2k}w_{j}^{-1}w_{s}^{-1})}\right]

\displaystyle H\left([u^{k-1}v^{k}t]\rho_{[W^{(i)}]^{-1},1,L};[u^{k-1}v^{k}t]\rho_{[W^{(j)}]^{-1},1,L}\right)=\prod_{w_{i}\in W^{(i)},w_{j}\in W^{(j)}}\frac{1}{(1-u^{2k-2}v^{2k}t^{2}w_{i}^{-1}w_{j}^{-1})}

\displaystyle H\left([u^{k-1}v^{k}t]\rho_{[W^{(i)}]^{-1},1,L};[u^{k-1}v^{k}]\{(-1)*\rho_{[W^{(j)}]^{-1},1,L}\}\right)=\prod_{w_{i}\in W^{(i)},w_{j}\in W^{(j)}}(1-u^{2k-2}v^{2k}tw_{i}^{-1}w_{j}^{-1})

\displaystyle H\left([u^{k-1}v^{k}]\{(-1)*\rho_{[W^{(i)}]^{-1},1,L}\};[u^{k-1}v^{k}]\{(-1)*\rho_{[W^{(j)}]^{-1},1,L}\}\right)=\prod_{w_{i}\in W^{(i)},w_{j}\in W^{(j)}}\frac{1}{(1-u^{2k-2}v^{2k}w_{i}^{-1}w_{j}^{-1})}

Then (
4.4
) follows.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}\left([u^{k}v^{k-1}]\rho_{W_{I}^{\circ}}\right)=\prod_{s\in[m]}\left[\widetilde{H}\left((-1)*[u^{k}v^{k-1}t^{-1}]\rho_{W^{(i_{s})},1,L}\right)\widetilde{H}\left([u^{k}v^{k-1}]\{\rho_{W^{(i_{s})},1,L}\}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\times\left[\prod_{1\leq s<g\leq m}H\left((-1)*[u^{k}v^{k-1}t^{-1}]\rho_{W^{(i_{s})},1,L};(-1)*[u^{k}v^{k-1}t^{-1}]\rho_{W^{(i_{g})},1,L}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\times\left[\prod_{s,g\in[m]}H\left((-1)*[u^{k}v^{k-1}t^{-1}]\rho_{W^{(i_{s})},1,L};[u^{k}v^{k-1}]\{\rho_{W^{(i_{g})},1,L}\}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\times\left[\prod_{1\leq s<g\leq m}H\left([u^{k}v^{k-1}]\{\rho_{W^{(i_{s})},1,L}\};[u^{k}v^{k-1}]\{\rho_{W^{(i_{g})},1,L}\}\right)\right]

We have

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}\left((-1)*[u^{k}v^{k-1}t^{-1}]\rho_{W^{(i)},1,L}\right)

\displaystyle=\left[\prod_{w\in W^{(i)}}\frac{1}{(1+u^{k}v^{k-1}t^{-1}w)}\right]\left[\prod_{w_{j},w_{s}\in W^{(i)},j<s}\frac{1}{(1-u^{2k}v^{2k-2}t^{-2}w_{j}w_{s})}\right]

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}\left([u^{k}v^{k-1}]\rho_{W^{(i)},1,L}\right)=\left[\prod_{w\in W^{(i)}}\frac{1}{(1-u^{k}v^{k-1}w)}\right]\left[\prod_{w_{j},w_{s}\in W^{(i)},j<s}\frac{1}{(1-u^{2k}v^{2k-2}w_{j}w_{s})}\right]

\displaystyle H\left((-1)*[u^{k}v^{k-1}t^{-1}]\rho_{W^{(i)},1,L};(-1)*[u^{k}v^{k-1}t^{-1}]\rho_{W^{(j)},1,L}\right)=\prod_{w_{i}\in W^{(i)},w_{j}\in W^{(j)}}\frac{1}{(1-u^{2k}v^{2k-2}t^{-2}w_{i}w_{j})}

\displaystyle H\left((-1)*[u^{k}v^{k-1}t^{-1}]\rho_{W^{(i)},1,L};[u^{k}v^{k-1}]\{\rho_{W^{(j)},1,L}\}\right)=\prod_{w_{i}\in W^{(i)},w_{j}\in W^{(j)}}(1-u^{2k}v^{2k-2}t^{-1}w_{i}w_{j})

\displaystyle H\left([u^{k}v^{k-1}]\rho_{W^{(i)},1,L};[u^{k}v^{k-1}]\rho_{W^{(j)},1,L}\right)=\prod_{w_{i}\in W^{(i)},w_{j}\in W^{(j)}}\frac{1}{(1-u^{2k}v^{2k-2}w_{i}w_{j})}

Then (
4.5
) follows.

Moreover,

\displaystyle H(u^{2k}\rho_{A};v^{2k}\rho_{W_{I}})

\displaystyle=\prod_{s\in[m]}\prod_{w\in W^{(i_{s})}}\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=-,a_{i}=L}\frac{1-u^{2k}v^{2k}x_{j}w^{-1}}{1-u^{2k}v^{2k}tx_{j}w^{-1}}\right]\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=-,a_{i}=R}\frac{1+u^{2k}v^{2k}tx_{j}w^{-1}}{1+u^{2k}v^{2k}x_{j}w^{-1}}\right]

\displaystyle H(u^{2k}\rho_{A};v^{2k-2}\rho_{W^{\circ}_{I}}))

\displaystyle=\prod_{s\in[m]}\prod_{w\in W^{(i_{s})}}\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=-,a_{i}=L}\frac{1-u^{2k}v^{2k-2}t^{-1}x_{j}w}{1-u^{2k}v^{2k-2}x_{j}w}\right]

\displaystyle\times\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=-,a_{i}=R}\frac{1+u^{2k}v^{2k-2}x_{j}w}{1+u^{2k}v^{2k-2}t^{-1}x_{j}w}\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{s\in[m]}\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=-,a_{i}=L}G_{1,<}(W^{(i_{s})},u^{2k}v^{2k-2}x_{j})\right]

\displaystyle\times\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=-,a_{i}=R}G_{0,<}(W^{(i_{s})},u^{2k}v^{2k-2}x_{j})\right]

\displaystyle H(u^{2k}\rho_{B};v^{2k}\rho_{W^{\circ}_{I}})

\displaystyle=\prod_{s\in[m]}\prod_{w\in W^{(i_{s})}}\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=+,a_{i}=L}\frac{1-u^{2k}v^{2k}t^{-1}x_{j}w}{1-u^{2k}v^{2k}x_{j}w}\right]\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=+,a_{i}=R}\frac{1+u^{2k}v^{2k}x_{j}w}{1+u^{2k}v^{2k}t^{-1}x_{j}w}\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{s\in[m]}\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=+,a_{i}=L}G_{1,<}(W^{(i_{s})},u^{2k}v^{2k}x_{j})\right]\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=+,a_{i}=R}G_{0,<}(W^{(i_{s})},u^{2k}v^{2k}x_{j})\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{s\in[m]}\prod_{w\in W^{(i_{s})}}\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=+,a_{i}=L}\frac{1-u^{2k-2}v^{2k}x_{j}w^{-1}}{1-u^{2k-2}v^{2k}tx_{j}w^{-1}}\right]

\displaystyle\times\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=+,a_{i}=R}\frac{1+u^{2k-2}v^{2k}tx_{j}w^{-1}}{1+u^{2k-2}v^{2k}x_{j}w^{-1}}\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{s\in[m]}\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=+,a_{i}=L}G_{1,>}(W^{(i_{s})},u^{2k-2}v^{2k}x_{j},t)\right]

\displaystyle\times\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=+,a_{i}=R}G_{0,>}(W^{(i_{s})},u^{2k-2}v^{2k}x_{j},t)\right]

\displaystyle H(u^{2k}\rho_{W_{I}};v^{2k}\rho_{W^{\circ}_{I}})

\displaystyle=\prod_{s\in[m]}\prod_{w_{s}\in W^{(i_{s})}}\prod_{g\in[m]}\prod_{w_{g}\in W^{(i_{g})}}\left[\frac{(1-u^{2k}v^{2k}[w_{s}]^{-1}w_{g})^{2}}{(1-u^{2k}v^{2k}t[w_{s}]^{-1}w_{g})(1-u^{2k}v^{2k}t^{-1}[w_{s}]^{-1}w_{g})}\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{s\in[m]}\prod_{g\in[m]}T_{L,L}(W^{(i_{s})},u^{2k}v^{2k}W^{(i_{g})})

∎

Let
\epsilon>0
be small and positive.
Under the periodicity Assumption
2.6
, when

\displaystyle\epsilon(r^{(\epsilon)}-l^{(\epsilon)})=(V_{m}-V_{0})+o(1),

for each
p\in[m]
,
j\in[n]
and
i\in[l^{(\epsilon)}..r^{(\epsilon)}]
, let

\displaystyle I^{(\epsilon)}_{j,p,>i,1}

\displaystyle=\left\{u\in\left[v_{p-1}^{(\epsilon)}+1,v_{p}^{(\epsilon)}\right]\cap\{n{\mathbb{Z}}+j\}\cap[i..r]:b_{u}=-,a_{u}=a_{i}\right\}

\displaystyle I^{(\epsilon)}_{j,p,<i,1}

\displaystyle=\left\{u\in\left[v_{p-1}^{(\epsilon)}+1,v_{p}^{(\epsilon)}\right]\cap\{n{\mathbb{Z}}+j\}\cap[l..i-1]:b_{u}=+,a_{u}=a_{i}\right\}

\displaystyle I^{(\epsilon)}_{j,p,>i,0}

\displaystyle=\left\{u\in\left[v_{p-1}^{(\epsilon)}+1,v_{p}^{(\epsilon)}\right]\cap\{n{\mathbb{Z}}+j\}\cap[i..r]:b_{u}=-,a_{u}\neq a_{i}\right\}

\displaystyle I^{(\epsilon)}_{j,p,<i,0}

\displaystyle=\left\{u\in\left[v_{p-1}^{(\epsilon)}+1,v_{p}^{(\epsilon)}\right]\cap\{n{\mathbb{Z}}+j\}\cap[l..i-1]:b_{u}=+,a_{u}\neq a_{i}\right\}

and let
|I^{(\epsilon)}_{j,p,>i,1}|
,
|I^{(\epsilon)}_{j,p,<i,1}|
,
|I^{(\epsilon)}_{j,p,>i,0}|
, and
|I^{(\epsilon)}_{j,p,<i,0}|
be their cardinalities, respectively.

It is straightforward to check the following lemma under Assumption
2.6
.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
j\in[n]
,
p\in[m]
be fixed. Let
\chi\in{\mathbb{R}}
, and let
\{i^{(\epsilon)}\in{\mathbb{Z}}\}_{\epsilon>0}
be a sequence such that

(4.6)

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\epsilon i^{(\epsilon)}=\chi.

Suppose that Assumption
2.6
holds.

(1)

either for all
\epsilon>0
and
\chi<V_{p}
,
I_{j,p,>i^{(\epsilon)},1}^{(\epsilon)}=\emptyset
; or for none of
\epsilon>0
and
\chi<V_{p}
,

I_{j,p,>i^{(\epsilon)},1}^{(\epsilon)}=\emptyset
.

(2)

either for all
\epsilon>0
and
\chi>V_{p-1}
,
I_{j,p,<i^{(\epsilon)},1}^{(\epsilon)}=\emptyset
; or for none of
\epsilon>0
and
\chi>V_{p-1}
,

I_{j,p,<i^{(\epsilon)},1}^{(\epsilon)}=\emptyset
.

(3)

either for all
\epsilon>0
and
\chi<V_{p}
,
I_{j,p,>i^{(\epsilon)},0}^{(\epsilon)}=\emptyset
; or for none of
\epsilon>0
and
\chi<V_{p}
,

I_{j,p,>i^{(\epsilon)},0}^{(\epsilon)}=\emptyset
.

(4)

either for all
\epsilon>0
and
\chi>V_{p-1}
,
I_{j,p,<i^{(\epsilon)},0}^{(\epsilon)}=\emptyset
; or for none of
\epsilon>0
and
\chi>V_{p-1}
,

I_{j,p,<i^{(\epsilon)},0}^{(\epsilon)}=\emptyset
.

We use
\mathcal{E}_{j,p,>,1}
to denote the event that for none of
\epsilon>0
and
\chi<V_{p}
,

I_{j,p,>i^{(\epsilon)},1}^{(\epsilon)}=\emptyset
;
\mathcal{E}_{j,p,<,1}
to denote the event that for none of
\epsilon>0
and
\chi>V_{p-1}
,

I_{j,p,<i^{(\epsilon)},1}^{(\epsilon)}=\emptyset
;
\mathcal{E}_{j,p,>,0}
to denote the event that for none of
\epsilon>0
and
\chi<V_{p}
,

I_{j,p,>i^{(\epsilon)},0}^{(\epsilon)}=\emptyset
;
\mathcal{E}_{j,p,<,0}
to denote the event that for none of
\epsilon>0
and
\chi>V_{p-1}
,

I_{j,p,<i^{(\epsilon)},0}^{(\epsilon)}=\emptyset
.

Lemma 4.4
.

Suppose Assumption
2.6
and (
4.6
) hold.
Let

\displaystyle G^{(\epsilon)}_{1,>i}(W)

\displaystyle=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[(l+1)^{(\epsilon)}..r^{(\epsilon)}],j\geq i\\
b_{j}=-,a_{j}=a_{i}\end{subarray}}G_{1,>}(W,x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t);\qquad G^{(\epsilon)}_{1,<i}(W)=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[l^{(\epsilon)}..r^{(\epsilon)}],j<i\\
b_{j}=+,a_{i}=a_{j}\end{subarray}}G_{1,<}(W,x^{(\epsilon)}_{j},t);

\displaystyle G^{(\epsilon)}_{0,>i}(W)

\displaystyle=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[(l+1)^{(\epsilon)}..r^{(\epsilon)}],j\geq i\\
b_{j}=-,a_{j}\neq a_{i}\end{subarray}}G_{0,>}(W,x^{(\epsilon)}_{j},t);\qquad G^{(\epsilon)}_{0,<i}(W)=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[l^{(\epsilon)}..r^{(\epsilon)}],j<i\\
b_{j}=+,a_{i}\neq a_{j}\end{subarray}}G_{0,<}(W,x^{\epsilon}_{j},t).

and

(4.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1,>\chi}(w):

\displaystyle=\prod_{(p\in[m],V_{p}>\chi)}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\left(\frac{1-\left[w\tau_{j}\right]^{-1}e^{V_{p}}}{1-e^{\max\{V_{p-1},\chi\}}\left[w\tau_{j}\right]^{-1}}\right)^{\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{j,p,>,1}}}

(4.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1,<\chi}(w):

\displaystyle=\prod_{(p\in[m],V_{p-1}<\chi)}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\left(\frac{1-we^{-V_{p-1}}\tau_{j}}{1-e^{-\min\{V_{p},\chi\}}w\tau_{j}}\right)^{\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{j,p,<,1}}}

(4.9)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{0,>\chi}(w):

\displaystyle=\prod_{(p\in[m],V_{p}>\chi)}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\left(\frac{1+e^{\max\{V_{p-1},\chi\}}\left[w\tau_{j}\right]^{-1}}{1+\left[w\tau_{j}\right]^{-1}e^{V_{p}}}\right)^{\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{j,p,>,0}}}

(4.10)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{0,<\chi}(w):

\displaystyle=\prod_{(p\in[m],V_{p-1}<\chi)}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\left(\frac{1+e^{-\min\{V_{p},\chi\}}w\tau_{j}}{1+we^{-V_{p-1}}\tau_{j}}\right)^{\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{j,p,<,0}}}.

Then

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}G^{(\epsilon)}_{1,>i^{(\epsilon)}}(W)

\displaystyle=\left[\prod_{w_{s}\in W}\mathcal{G}_{1,>\chi}(w_{s})\right]^{\beta},\quad\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}G^{(\epsilon)}_{1<i^{(\epsilon)}}(W)=\left[\prod_{w_{s}\in W}\mathcal{G}_{1,<\chi}(w_{s})\right]^{\beta},

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}G^{(\epsilon)}_{0,>i^{(\epsilon)}}(W)

\displaystyle=\left[\prod_{w_{s}\in W}\mathcal{G}_{0,>\chi}(w_{s})\right]^{\beta},\quad\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}G^{(\epsilon)}_{0,<i^{(\epsilon)}}(W)=\left[\prod_{w_{s}\in W}\mathcal{G}_{0,<\chi}(w_{s})\right]^{\beta}.

Here the logarithmic branches for
\mathcal{G}_{1,>\chi}
,
\mathcal{G}_{1,<\chi}
,
\mathcal{G}_{0,>\chi}
,
\mathcal{G}_{0,<\chi}
are chosen so that when
z
approaches the positive real axis, the imaginary part of
\log z
approaches 0.

Proof.

See Lemma 5.6 of
[
32
]
.
∎

Note that

(1)

For
\mathcal{G}_{1,>\chi}(w)
, the condition that the denominator vanishes gives

\displaystyle w\in\left\{e^{\max\{V_{p-1},\chi\}}\tau_{j}^{-1}\right\}_{V_{p}>\chi,j\in[n]\ \mathrm{s.t.}\ \mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{j,p,>,1}}=1}:=\mathcal{R}_{\chi,1,1};

the condition that the numerator vanishes gives

\displaystyle w\in\left\{e^{V_{p}}\tau_{j}^{-1}\right\}_{V_{p}>\chi,j\in[n]\ \mathrm{s.t.}\ \mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{j,p,>,1}}=1}:=\mathcal{R}_{\chi,1,2};

Hence for each
\chi\in(l^{(0)},r^{(0)})
, and
\chi\notin\{V_{i}\}_{i\in[m]}
,
\mathcal{G}_{1,>\chi}(w)
has zeros given by
\mathcal{R}_{\chi,1,2}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{\chi,1,1}

and poles given by
\mathcal{R}_{\chi,1,1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{\chi,1,2}
.

(2)

For
\mathcal{G}_{1,<\chi}(w)
, the condition that the denominator vanishes gives

\displaystyle w\in\left\{e^{\min\{V_{p},\chi\}}\tau_{j}^{-1}\right\}_{V_{p-1}<\chi,j\in[n]\ \mathrm{s.t.}\ \mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{j,p,<,1}}=1}:=\mathcal{R}_{\chi,2,1}

the condition that the numerator vanishes gives

\displaystyle w\in\left\{e^{V_{p-1}}\tau_{j}^{-1}\right\}_{V_{p-1}<\chi,j\in[n]\ \mathrm{s.t.}\ \mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{j,p,<,1}}=1}:=\mathcal{R}_{\chi,2,2},

Hence for each
\chi\in(l^{(0)},r^{(0)})
, and
\chi\notin\{V_{i}\}_{i\in[m]}
,
\mathcal{G}_{1,<\chi}(w)
has zeros given by

\mathcal{R}_{\chi,2,2}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{\chi,2,1}

and poles given by
\mathcal{R}_{\chi,2,1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{\chi,2,2}
;

(3)

For
\mathcal{G}_{0,>\chi}(w)
, the condition that the denominator vanishes gives

\displaystyle w\in\left\{-e^{V_{p}}\tau_{j}^{-1}\right\}_{V_{p}>\chi,j\in[n]\ \mathrm{s.t.}\ \mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{j,p,>,0}}=1}:=\mathcal{R}_{\chi,3,1},

the condition that the numerator vanishes gives

\displaystyle w\in\left\{-e^{\max\{V_{p-1},\chi\}}\tau_{j}^{-1}\right\}_{V_{p}>\chi,j\in[n]\mathrm{s.t.}\ \mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{j,p,>,0}}=1}:=\mathcal{R}_{\chi,3,2},

Hence for each
\chi\in(l^{(0)},r^{(0)})
, and
\chi\notin\{V_{i}\}_{i\in[m]}
,
\mathcal{G}_{0,>\chi}(w)
has zeros given by

\mathcal{R}_{\chi,3,2}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{\chi,3,1}

and poles
\mathcal{R}_{\chi,3,1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{\chi,3,2}
.

(4)

For
\mathcal{G}_{0,<\chi}(w)
, the condition that the denominator vanishes gives

\displaystyle w\in\left\{-e^{V_{p-1}}\tau_{j}^{-1}\right\}_{V_{p-1}<\chi,j\in[n]\ \mathrm{s.t.}\ \mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{j,p,<,0}}=1}:=\mathcal{R}_{\chi,4,1},

the condition that the numerator vanishes gives

\displaystyle w\in\left\{-e^{\min\{V_{p},\chi\}}\tau_{j}^{-1}\right\}_{V_{p-1}<\chi,j\in[n]\ \mathrm{s.t.}\ \mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{j,p,<,0}}=1}:=\mathcal{R}_{\chi,4,2},

Hence for each
\chi\in(l^{(0)},r^{(0)})
, and
\chi\notin\{V_{i}\}_{i\in[m]}
,
\mathcal{G}_{0,>\chi}(w_{s})
has zeros given by
\mathcal{R}_{\chi,4,2}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{\chi,4,1}
, and poles given by
\mathcal{R}_{\chi,4,1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{\chi,4,2}
.

(5)

For
\mathcal{G}_{1,>V_{0}}(u^{-2k_{1}}v^{-2k_{2}}w)
, the condition that the denominator vanishes gives

\displaystyle w\in\left\{u^{2k_{1}}v^{2k_{2}}e^{V_{p-1}}\tau_{j}^{-1}\right\}_{p\in[m],j\in[n]\ \mathrm{s.t.}\ \mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{j,p,>,1}}=1}:=\mathcal{R}_{5,1,k_{1},k_{2}},

the condition that the numerator vanishes gives

\displaystyle w\in\left\{u^{2k_{1}}v^{2k_{2}}e^{V_{p}}\tau_{j}^{-1}\right\}_{V_{p}>\chi,j\in[n]\ \mathrm{s.t.}\ \mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{j,p,>,1}}=1}:=\mathcal{R}_{5,2,k_{1},k_{2}},

\mathcal{G}_{1,V_{0}}(u^{-2k_{1}}v^{-2k_{2}}w)

\mathcal{R}_{5,2,k_{1},k_{2}}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{5,1,k_{1},k_{2}}

and poles given by
\mathcal{R}_{5,1,k_{1},k_{2}}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{5,2,k_{1},k_{2}}
.

(6)

For
\mathcal{G}_{1,<V_{m}}(u^{2k_{1}}v^{2k_{2}}w)
, the condition that the denominator vanishes gives

\displaystyle w\in\left\{u^{-2k_{1}}v^{-2k_{2}}e^{V_{p}}\tau_{j}^{-1}\right\}_{p\in[m],j\in[n]\ \mathrm{s.t.}\ \mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{j,p,<,1}}=1}:=\mathcal{R}_{6,1,k_{1},k_{2}},\

the condition that the numerator vanishes gives

\displaystyle w\in\left\{e^{u^{-2k_{1}}v^{-2k_{2}}V_{p-1}}\tau_{j}^{-1}\right\}_{p\in[m],j\in[n]\ \mathrm{s.t.}\ \mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{j,p,<,1}}=1}:=\mathcal{R}_{6,2,k_{1},k_{2}};

Hence
\mathcal{G}_{1,<\chi}(u^{2k_{1}}v^{2k_{2}}w)
has zeros given by

\mathcal{R}_{6,2,k_{1},k_{2}}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{6,1,k_{1},k_{2}}

and poles given by
\mathcal{R}_{6,1,k_{1},k_{2}}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{6,2,k_{1},k_{2}}
;

(7)

For
\mathcal{G}_{0,>V_{0}}(u^{-2k_{1}}v^{-2k_{2}}w)
, the condition that the denominator vanishes gives

\displaystyle w\in\left\{-u^{2k_{1}}v^{2k_{2}}e^{V_{p}}\tau_{j}^{-1}\right\}_{p\in[m],j\in[n]\ \mathrm{s.t.}\ \mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{j,p,>,0}}=1}:=\mathcal{R}_{7,1,k_{1},k_{2}},

the condition that the numerator vanishes gives

\displaystyle w\in\left\{-e^{V_{p-1}}\tau_{j}^{-1}\right\}_{p\in[m],j\in[n]\mathrm{s.t.}\ \mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{j,p,>,0}}=1}:=\mathcal{R}_{7,2,k_{1},k_{2}},

Hence
\mathcal{G}_{0,>V_{0}}(u^{-2k_{1}}v^{-2k_{2}}w)
has zeros given by

\mathcal{R}_{7,2,k_{1},k_{2}}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{7,1,k_{1},k_{2}}

and poles
\mathcal{R}_{7,1,k_{1},k_{2}}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{7,2,k_{1},k_{2}}
.

(8)

For
\mathcal{G}_{0,<V_{m}}(u^{2k_{1}}v^{2k_{2}}w)
, the condition that the denominator vanishes gives

\displaystyle w\in\left\{-u^{-2k_{1}}v^{-2k_{2}}e^{V_{p-1}}\tau_{j}^{-1}\right\}_{p\in[m],j\in[n]\ \mathrm{s.t.}\ \mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{j,p,<,0}}=1}:=\mathcal{R}_{8,1,k_{1},k_{2}},\ \forall s\in[k]

the condition that the numerator vanishes gives

\displaystyle w\in\left\{-u^{-2k_{1}}v^{-2k_{2}}e^{V_{p}}\tau_{j}^{-1}\right\}_{p\in[m],j\in[n]\ \mathrm{s.t.}\ \mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{j,p,<,0}}=1}:=\mathcal{R}_{8,2,k_{1},k_{2}},\ \forall s\in[k]

Hence
\mathcal{G}_{0,>\chi}(u^{2k_{1}}v^{2k_{2}}w)
has zeros given by
\mathcal{R}_{8,2,k_{1},k_{2}}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{8,1,k_{1},k_{2}}
, and poles given by
\mathcal{R}_{8,1,k_{1},k_{2}}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{8,2,k_{1},k_{2}}
.

Proposition 4.5
.

Suppose Assumptions
2.6
and (
4.6
) hold. Assume
a_{i^{(\epsilon)}}=L
for all
\epsilon>0
. Let
\mathrm{Pr}^{(\epsilon)}
be the corresponding probability measure. Then

(4.11)

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{Pr}^{(\epsilon)}}\left[\gamma_{g}(\lambda^{(M,i^{(\epsilon)})};t,t)\right]=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathbf{i}}\oint_{\mathcal{C}}\frac{dw}{w}\left[\mathcal{G}_{\chi}(w)\right]^{g\beta}\prod_{k\geq 1}\left[\mathcal{F}_{u,v,k}(w)\right]^{g\beta}.

where
g\geq 0
is an integer,

(4.12)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\chi}(w):

\displaystyle=\mathcal{G}_{1,>\chi}(w)\mathcal{G}_{1,<\chi}(w)\mathcal{G}_{0,>\chi}(w)\mathcal{G}_{0,<\chi}(w)

and

(4.13)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{u,v,k}(w):

\displaystyle=\mathcal{G}_{1,>V_{0}}(u^{-2k}v^{-2k}w)\mathcal{G}_{1,<V_{m}}(u^{2k}v^{2k}w)\mathcal{G}_{0,>V_{0}}(u^{-2k}v^{-2k}w)\mathcal{G}_{0,<V_{m}}(u^{2k}v^{2k}w)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1,>V_{0}}(u^{2-2k}v^{-2k}w)\mathcal{G}_{1,<V_{m}}(u^{2k}v^{2k-2}w)\mathcal{G}_{0,>V_{0}}(u^{2-2k}v^{-2k}w)\mathcal{G}_{0,<V_{m}}(u^{2k}v^{2k-2}w);

the contour
\mathcal{C}
is positively oriented (which may be a union of disjoint simple closed curves) enclosing
0
and every point in

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\chi}:=[\mathcal{R}_{\chi,1,1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{\chi,1,2}]\cup[\mathcal{R}_{\chi,3,1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{\chi,3,2}]\cup

\displaystyle[\cup_{k\geq 1}\left([\mathcal{R}_{5,1,k,k}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{5,2,k,k}]\cup[\mathcal{R}_{7,1,k,k}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{7,2,k,k}]\cup[\mathcal{R}_{5,1,k-1,k}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{5,2,k-1,k}]\cup[\mathcal{R}_{7,1,k-1,k}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{7,2,k-1,k}]\right)]

but does not enclose any other zeros or poles of

\mathcal{G}_{\chi}(w)
; the expression

\displaystyle F^{k\beta}=e^{k\beta\log F}

where the branch of
\log F
is the one which takes positive real values when
F
is positive and real.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{Pr}^{(\epsilon)}}\left[\gamma_{g}(\lambda^{(M,i^{(\epsilon)})};t,t)\right]=\oint_{\mathcal{C}_{i,g}^{(\epsilon)}}\ldots\oint_{\mathcal{C}_{i,1}^{(\epsilon)}}\left[D(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})};t,t)\right.

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[i..r]\\
b_{j}=-,a_{j}=L\end{subarray}}G^{(\epsilon)}_{1,>}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[i_{s}..r]\\
b_{j}=-,a_{j}=R\end{subarray}}G^{(\epsilon)}_{0,>}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[l..i-1]\\
b_{j}=+,a_{i}=L\end{subarray}}G^{(\epsilon)}_{1,<}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[l..i-1]\\
b_{j}=+,a_{i}=R\end{subarray}}G^{(\epsilon)}_{0,<}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)

\displaystyle\left(\prod_{k\geq 1}\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=-,a_{i}=L}G^{(\epsilon)}_{1,>}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},u^{2k}v^{2k}x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)G^{(\epsilon)}_{1,<}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},u^{2k}v^{2k-2}x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=-,a_{i}=R}G^{(\epsilon)}_{0,>}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},u^{2k}v^{2k}x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)G_{0,<}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},u^{2k}v^{2k-2}x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)\right]

\displaystyle\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=+,a_{i}=L}G^{(\epsilon)}_{1,<}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},u^{2k}v^{2k}x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)G^{(\epsilon)}_{1,>}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},u^{2k-2}v^{2k}x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)\right]

\displaystyle\left.\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=+,a_{i}=R}G^{(\epsilon)}_{0,<}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},u^{2k}v^{2k}x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)G^{(\epsilon)}_{0,>}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},u^{2k-2}v^{2k}x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)\right]\right)

\displaystyle\prod_{k\geq 1}\left(\widetilde{H}((-1)*[u^{k}v^{k-1}\rho_{[W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})}]^{-1},t^{-1},2,a_{i}}])\widetilde{H}([u^{k-1}v^{k}]\rho_{W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},t,2,a_{i}})T_{L,L}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},u^{2k}v^{2k}W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})})\right),

where for
1\leq j\leq k
,
\mathcal{C}_{i,j}^{(\epsilon)}
is the integral contour for
w^{(i)}_{j}\in W^{(i)}
, and
\mathcal{C}_{i,1}^{(\epsilon)},\ldots,\mathcal{C}_{i,k}^{(\epsilon)}
satisfy the conditions as described in Lemma
3.8
. As
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
, assume that

\mathcal{C}_{i,1}^{(\epsilon)},\ldots,\mathcal{C}_{i,k}^{(\epsilon)}
converge to contours
\mathcal{C}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{C}_{k}
, respectively, such that
\mathcal{C}_{1},\ldots\mathcal{C}_{k}
are separated from one another and do not cross any of the singularities of the integrand, by Lemma
4.4
, we have that

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[i..r]\\
b_{j}=-,a_{j}=L\end{subarray}}G^{(\epsilon)}_{1,>}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[i_{s}..r]\\
b_{j}=-,a_{j}=R\end{subarray}}G^{(\epsilon)}_{0,>}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[l..i-1]\\
b_{j}=+,a_{i}=L\end{subarray}}G^{(\epsilon)}_{1,<}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[l..i-1]\\
b_{j}=+,a_{i}=R\end{subarray}}G^{(\epsilon)}_{0,<}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)

\displaystyle=\prod_{s\in[g]}\left[\mathcal{G}_{1,>\chi}(w_{s})\mathcal{G}_{1,<\chi}(w_{s})\mathcal{G}_{0,>\chi}(w_{s})\mathcal{G}_{0,<\chi}(w_{s})\right]^{\beta}.

and for each integer
k\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[l..r]\\
b_{j}=-,a_{j}=L\end{subarray}}G^{(\epsilon)}_{1,>}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},u^{2k}v^{2k}x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[l..r]\\
b_{j}=-,a_{j}=R\end{subarray}}G^{(\epsilon)}_{0,>}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},u^{2k}v^{2k}x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[l..r]\\
b_{j}=+,a_{i}=L\end{subarray}}G^{(\epsilon)}_{1,<}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},u^{2k}v^{2k}x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[l..r]\\
b_{j}=+,a_{i}=R\end{subarray}}G^{(\epsilon)}_{0,<}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},u^{2k}v^{2k}x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)

\displaystyle=\prod_{s\in[g]}\left[\mathcal{G}_{1,>V_{0}}(u^{-2k}v^{-2k}w_{s})\mathcal{G}_{1,<V_{m}}(u^{2k}v^{2k}w_{s})\mathcal{G}_{0,>V_{0}}(u^{-2k}v^{-2k}w_{s})\mathcal{G}_{0,<V_{m}}(u^{2k}v^{2k}w_{s})\right]^{\beta}.

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=-,a_{i}=L}G^{(\epsilon)}_{1,<}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},u^{2k}v^{2k-2}x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)\right]\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=-,a_{i}=R}G_{0,<}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},u^{2k}v^{2k-2}x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)\right]

\displaystyle\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=+,a_{i}=L}G^{(\epsilon)}_{1,>}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},u^{2k-2}v^{2k}x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)\right]\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=+,a_{i}=R}G^{(\epsilon)}_{0,>}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},u^{2k-2}v^{2k}x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{s\in[g]}\left[\mathcal{G}_{1,>V_{0}}(u^{2-2k}v^{-2k}w_{s})\mathcal{G}_{1,<V_{m}}(u^{2k}v^{2k-2}w_{s})\mathcal{G}_{0,>V_{0}}(u^{2-2k}v^{-2k}w_{s})\mathcal{G}_{0,<V_{m}}(u^{2k}v^{2k-2}w_{s})\right]^{\beta}.

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}T_{L,L}(W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},u^{2k}v^{2k}W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})})=\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\prod_{i\in[g]}\prod_{j\in[g]}\frac{\left(w_{i}-u^{2k}v^{2k}w_{j}\right)^{2}}{\left(w_{i}-t^{-1}u^{2k}v^{2k}w_{j}\right)\left(w_{i}-tu^{2k}v^{2k}w_{j}\right)}=1.

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}D(W^{(i^{\epsilon})};t,t)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathbf{i})^{g}}\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac{1}{w_{i}}}{\left(w_{2}-w_{1}\right)\ldots\left(w_{k}-w_{k-1}\right)}\prod_{i=1}^{k}dw_{i}

By (
4.4
) and (
4.5
) we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\widetilde{H}([u^{k-1}v^{k}]\rho_{W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})},t,2,a_{i}})=1

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\widetilde{H}((-1)*[u^{k}v^{k-1}\rho_{[W^{(i^{(\epsilon)})}]^{-1},t^{-1},2,a_{i}}])=1

Then (
4.11
) follows from Lemmas
A.7
.
∎

Remark 4.6
.

When
u=v=0
, from (
4.11
) we recover Proposition 5.7 of
[
32
]
for the limit shape of pure dimer coverings on rail yard graphs.

When
u\neq 0
and
v=0
, from (
4.11
) we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{Pr}^{(\epsilon)}}\left[\gamma_{g}(\lambda^{(M,i^{(\epsilon)})};t,t)\right]=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathbf{i}}\oint_{\mathcal{C}}\frac{dw}{w}\left[\mathcal{G}_{1,>\chi}(w)\mathcal{G}_{1,<\chi}(w)\mathcal{G}_{0,>\chi}(w)\mathcal{G}_{0,<\chi}(w)\right]^{g\beta}

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{G}_{1,<V_{m}}(u^{2}w)\mathcal{G}_{0,<V_{m}}(u^{2}w)\right]^{g\beta};

which gives the limit shape of dimer coverings on rail yard graphs with free boundary condition on the left, and empty boundary condition on the right.

Theorem 4.7
.

Suppose Assumption
2.6
holds. Let
s
be a positive integer such that for all
d\in[s]
,
\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\epsilon i_{d}^{(\epsilon)}=\chi_{d},
,
such that
\chi_{1}\leq\chi_{2}\leq\ldots\leq\chi_{s}
. Assume that

(4.14)

\displaystyle a_{i_{1}^{(\epsilon)}}=a_{i_{2}^{(\epsilon)}}=\ldots=a_{i_{s}^{(\epsilon)}}=L.

Let
\mathrm{Pr}^{(\epsilon)}
be the corresponding probability measure and

\displaystyle Q_{g_{d}}^{(\epsilon)}(\epsilon i_{d}^{(\epsilon)}):=\frac{1}{\epsilon}\left(\gamma_{g_{d}}(\lambda^{(M,i_{d}^{(\epsilon)})};t,t)-\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{Pr}^{(\epsilon)}}(\gamma_{g_{d}}(\lambda^{(M,i_{d}^{(\epsilon)})};t,t)\right);\qquad\forall d\in[s]

Then

(Q_{g_{1}}^{(\epsilon)}(\epsilon i_{1}^{(\epsilon)}),\dots,Q_{g_{s}}^{(\epsilon)}(\epsilon i_{s}^{(\epsilon)}))
converges in distribution to the centered Gaussian vector

\displaystyle(Q_{g_{1}}(\chi_{1}),\ldots,Q_{g_{s}}(\chi_{s}))

as
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
, whose covariances are

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cov}\left[Q_{g_{d}}(\chi_{d}),Q_{g_{h}}(\chi_{h})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}\beta^{2}g_{d}g_{h}}{(2\pi\mathbf{i})^{2}}\oint_{\mathcal{C}_{d}}\oint_{\mathcal{C}_{h}}\frac{\left[\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{d}}(z)\right]^{g_{d}\beta}\left[\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{h}}(w)\right]^{g_{h}\beta}}{(z-w)^{2}}\prod_{k\geq 1}\left[\mathcal{F}_{u,v,k}(z)\right]^{g_{d}\beta}\prod_{k\geq 1}\left[\mathcal{F}_{u,v,k}(w)\right]^{g_{h}\beta}dzdw

where

•

1\leq d<h\leq s
; and

•

the
z
-contour is positively oriented enclosing
0
and every point in
\mathcal{D}_{\chi_{d}}
, but does not enclose any other zeros or poles of

\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{d}}(z)\prod_{k\geq 1}F_{u,v,k}(z)
; and

•

the
w
-contour is positively oriented enclosing
0
and every point in
\mathcal{D}_{\chi_{h}}
, but does not enclose any other zeros or poles of
\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{h}}(w)\prod_{k\geq 1}F_{u,v,k}(w)
; and

•

the
z
-contour and the
w
-contour are disjoint;

•

the branch of logarithmic function is chosen to take positive real values along the positive real axis.

To prove Theorem
4.7
, we shall compute the moments of
Q_{g_{d}}^{(\epsilon)}(\epsilon i_{d}^{(\epsilon)})
and show that these moments satisfy the Wick’s formula in the limit as
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
. We start with the following lemma about covariance.

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
d,h\in[s]
. Under the assumptions of Theorem
4.7
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\mathrm{Cov}\left[Q_{g_{d}}^{(\epsilon)}(\epsilon i_{d}^{(\epsilon)}),Q_{g_{h}}^{(\epsilon)}(\epsilon i_{h}^{(\epsilon)})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}\beta^{2}g_{d}g_{h}}{(2\pi\mathbf{i})^{2}}\oint_{\mathcal{C}_{d}}\oint_{\mathcal{C}_{h}}\frac{\left[\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{d}}(z)\right]^{g_{d}\beta}\left[\mathcal{G}_{\chi_{h}}(w)\right]^{g_{h}\beta}}{(z-w)^{2}}\prod_{k\geq 1}\left[\mathcal{F}_{u,v,k}(z)\right]^{g_{d}\beta}\prod_{k\geq 1}\left[\mathcal{F}_{u,v,k}(w)\right]^{g_{h}\beta}dzdw

where the
z
-contour
\mathcal{C}_{d}
and the
w
-contour
\mathcal{C}_{h}
satisfy the assumptions of Theorem
4.7
.

Proof.

Note that when
\mathrm{Cov}\left[Q_{g_{d}}^{(\epsilon)}(\epsilon i_{d}^{(\epsilon)})Q_{g_{h}}^{(\epsilon)}(\epsilon i_{h}^{(\epsilon)})\right]
is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\gamma_{g_{d}}(\lambda^{(M,i_{d}^{(\epsilon)})};t,t)\gamma_{g_{h}}(\lambda^{(M,i_{h}^{(\epsilon)})};t,t)\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\gamma_{g_{d}}(\lambda^{(M,i_{d}^{(\epsilon)})};t,t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{Pr}}\left[\gamma_{g_{h}}(\lambda^{(M,i_{h}^{(\epsilon)})};t,t)\right]}{\epsilon^{2}}

For
W=(w_{1},\dots,w_{g})
we use abbreviation

\displaystyle F_{i}^{(\epsilon)}(W)

\displaystyle=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[i..r]\\
b_{j}=-,a_{j}=L\end{subarray}}G^{(\epsilon)}_{1,>}(W,x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[i_{s}..r]\\
b_{j}=-,a_{j}=R\end{subarray}}G^{(\epsilon)}_{0,>}(W,x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[l..i-1]\\
b_{j}=+,a_{i}=L\end{subarray}}G^{(\epsilon)}_{1,<}(W,x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[l..i-1]\\
b_{j}=+,a_{i}=R\end{subarray}}G^{(\epsilon)}_{0,<}(W,x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)

\displaystyle\left(\prod_{k\geq 1}\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=-,a_{i}=L}G^{(\epsilon)}_{1,>}(W,u^{2k}v^{2k}x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)G^{(\epsilon)}_{1,<}(W,u^{2k}v^{2k-2}x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=-,a_{i}=R}G^{(\epsilon)}_{0,>}(W,u^{2k}v^{2k}x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)G_{0,<}(W,u^{2k}v^{2k-2}x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)\right]

\displaystyle\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=+,a_{i}=L}G^{(\epsilon)}_{1,<}(W,u^{2k}v^{2k}x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)G^{(\epsilon)}_{1,>}(W,u^{2k-2}v^{2k}x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)\right]

\displaystyle\left.\left[\prod_{j\in[l..r],b_{j}=+,a_{i}=R}G^{(\epsilon)}_{0,<}(W,u^{2k}v^{2k}x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)G^{(\epsilon)}_{0,>}(W,u^{2k-2}v^{2k}x_{j}^{(\epsilon)},t)\right]\right)

\displaystyle\prod_{k\geq 1}\left(\widetilde{H}((-1)*[u^{k}v^{k-1}\rho_{[W]^{-1},t^{-1},2,a_{i}}])\widetilde{H}([u^{k-1}v^{k}]\rho_{W,t,2,a_{i}})T_{L,L}(W,u^{2k}v^{2k}W)\right),

\displaystyle\times\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{g}\frac{1}{w_{j}}}{\left(w_{2}-w_{1}\right)\ldots\left(w_{g}-w_{g-1}\right)}\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq g}\frac{\left(1-\frac{w_{i}}{w_{j}}\right)^{2}}{\left(1-\frac{w_{i}}{tw_{j}}\right)\left(1-\frac{tw_{i}}{w_{j}}\right)}

Note that for
\epsilon i^{(\epsilon)}\to\chi
and
t\to 1

(4.15)

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}F_{i^{(\epsilon)}}^{(\epsilon)}(W)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{k}\frac{1}{w_{j}}}{\left(w_{2}-w_{1}\right)\ldots\left(w_{k}-w_{k-1}\right)}\prod_{s\in[g]}\left(\left[\mathcal{G}_{1,>\chi}(w_{s})\mathcal{G}_{1,<\chi}(w_{s})\mathcal{G}_{0,>\chi}(w_{s})\mathcal{G}_{0,<\chi}(w_{s})\right]^{\beta}\right.

\displaystyle\times\prod_{k\geq 1}\left[\mathcal{G}_{1,>V_{0}}(u^{-2k}v^{-2k}w_{s})\mathcal{G}_{1,<V_{m}}(u^{2k}v^{2k}w_{s})\mathcal{G}_{0,>V_{0}}(u^{-2k}v^{-2k}w_{s})\mathcal{G}_{0,<V_{m}}(u^{2k}v^{2k}w_{s})\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\mathcal{G}_{1,>V_{0}}(u^{2-2k}v^{-2k}w_{s})\mathcal{G}_{1,<V_{m}}(u^{2k}v^{2k-2}w_{s})\mathcal{G}_{0,>V_{0}}(u^{2-2k}v^{-2k}w_{s})\mathcal{G}_{0,<V_{m}}(u^{2k}v^{2k-2}w_{s})\right]^{\beta}\right)

By Lemma
4.2
, we obtain that
\mathrm{Cov}\left[Q_{g_{d}}^{(\epsilon)}(\epsilon i_{d}^{(\epsilon)}),Q_{g_{h}}^{(\epsilon)}(\epsilon i_{h}^{(\epsilon)})\right]

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}(2\pi\mathbf{i})^{g_{h}+g_{d}}}\oint_{\mathcal{C}_{1,1}^{(\epsilon)}}\ldots\oint_{\mathcal{C}_{1,g_{d}}^{(\epsilon)}}\oint_{\mathcal{C}_{2,1}^{(\epsilon)}}\ldots\oint_{\mathcal{C}_{2,g_{h}}^{(\epsilon)}}

\displaystyle\prod_{\xi\in\{{d,h}\}}\prod_{i=1}^{g_{\xi}}dw^{(i_{\xi}^{(\epsilon)})}_{i}F^{(\epsilon)}_{i_{\xi}^{(\epsilon)}}(W^{(i_{\xi}^{(\epsilon)})})

\displaystyle\times\left[T_{L,L}(W^{(i_{d}^{(\epsilon)})},W^{(i_{h}^{(\epsilon)})})-1\right],

where
T_{L,L}(Z,W)
is given by (
4.3
),
|W^{(i_{d}^{(\epsilon)})}|=k_{d}
,
|W^{(i_{h}^{(\epsilon)}}|=k_{h}
and for
1\leq i\leq k_{d}
(resp.

),
\mathcal{C}_{1,i}^{(\epsilon)}
(resp.
\mathcal{C}_{2,j}^{(\epsilon)}
) is the integral contour for
w_{i}^{(i_{d}^{(\epsilon)})}
(resp.
w_{j}^{(i_{h}^{(\epsilon)})}

), and
\mathcal{C}_{1,1}^{(\epsilon)},\ldots,\mathcal{C}_{1,k_{d}}^{(\epsilon)}
,

\mathcal{C}_{2,1}^{(\epsilon)},\ldots,\mathcal{C}_{2,k_{h}}^{(\epsilon)}

satisfy the conditions as described in Lemma
3.8
. As
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
, assume that

\mathcal{C}_{1}^{(\epsilon)},\ldots,\mathcal{C}_{k}^{(\epsilon)}
converge to contours
\mathcal{C}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{C}_{k}
, respectively, such that
\mathcal{C}_{1},\ldots\mathcal{C}_{k}
are separated from one another and do not cross any of the singularities of the integrand.

Using the same arguments as in the proof of Lemma 5.9 in
[
32
]
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\left[T_{L,L}(W^{(i_{d}^{(\epsilon)})},W^{(i_{h}^{(\epsilon)})})-1\right]

\displaystyle=n^{2}\beta^{2}\left[\sum_{(u,v)\in[g_{d}]\times[g_{h}]}\frac{w_{u}^{(i_{d}^{(\epsilon)})}w_{v}^{(i_{h}^{(\epsilon)})}}{(w_{u}^{(i_{d}^{(\epsilon)})}-t^{-1}w_{v}^{(i_{h}^{(\epsilon)})})(w_{u}^{(i_{d}^{(\epsilon)})}-tw_{v}^{(i_{h}^{(\epsilon)})})}\right]+o(\epsilon).

Then the lemma follows from Lemma
A.7
by similar arguments as in the proof of Lemma 5.6 in
[
32
]
.
∎

Lemma 4.9
.

Assume (
4.14
) holds.

(1)

Let
s\in{\mathbb{N}}
be odd, and
s\geq 3
. Then

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{Pr}^{(\epsilon)}}\left[\prod_{u=1}^{s}Q_{g_{u}}^{(\epsilon)}(\epsilon i_{u}^{(\epsilon)})\right]=0.

(2)

If
s\in{\mathbb{N}}
is even, then

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{Pr}^{(\epsilon)}}\left[\prod_{u=1}^{s}Q_{g_{u}}^{(\epsilon)}(\epsilon i_{u}^{(\epsilon)})\right]=\sum_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{s}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{Pr}^{(\epsilon)}}\prod_{u,v\in P}\left[Q_{g_{u}}^{(\epsilon)}(\epsilon i_{u}^{(\epsilon)})Q_{g_{v}}^{(\epsilon)}(\epsilon i_{v}^{(\epsilon)})\right]

where the sum runs over all pairings of
[s]
.

Proof.

The lemma follows from the same arguments as the proof of Lemma 5.7 in
[
32
]
.
∎

5.
Limit Shape and Frozen Boundary

In this section, we prove an integral formula for the Laplace transform of the rescaled height function (see Lemma
2.7
), which turns out to be deterministic, as a 2D analog of law of large numbers. From this we study the density of certain type of dimers via the Stieltjes transform, and identify the frozen region, the liquid region and the frozen boundary.

Proof of Theorem
2.7
.

Same arguments as in the proof of Lemma 6.1 in
[
32
]
. The idea is to first prove the expectation of the left hand side is the same as the right hand side, and then prove the covariance vanishes as
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
using Lemma
4.8
.

\Box

We shall establish conditions under which (
5.9
) has at most one pair of complex conjugate roots.

Assumption 5.1
.

Let
K\geq 1
be an arbitrary finite positive integer. Suppose that all the following conditions hold.

•

For each fixed
i,j,k_{1},k_{2},\chi
,

–

points in
\mathcal{R}_{\chi,i,j}
represented by different
(j,p)\in[n]\times[m]
are distinct; and

–

points in
\mathcal{R}_{i,j,k_{1},k_{2}}
represented by different
(j,p)\in[n]\times[m]
are distinct.

•

Between each two consecutive points in

\displaystyle D_{1,K}:=[\mathcal{R}_{\chi,1,1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{\chi,1,2}]\cup[\mathcal{R}_{\chi,3,1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{\chi,3,2}]\cup

\displaystyle[\cup_{k\in[K]}\left([\mathcal{R}_{5,1,k,k}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{5,2,k,k}]\cup[\mathcal{R}_{7,1,k,k}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{7,2,k,k}]\cup[\mathcal{R}_{5,1,k-1,k}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{5,2,k-1,k}]\cup[\mathcal{R}_{7,1,k-1,k}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{7,2,k-1,k}]\right)]

there is a unique point in

\displaystyle[\cup_{k\in[K]}\left([\mathcal{R}_{5,2,k,k}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{5,1,k,k}]\cup[\mathcal{R}_{7,2,k,k}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{7,1,k,k}]\cup[\mathcal{R}_{5,2,k-1,k}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{5,1,k-1,k}]\cup[\mathcal{R}_{7,2,k-1,k}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{7,1,k-1,k}]\right)]

and

let

between each two consecutive points in

\displaystyle D_{3,K}:=[\mathcal{R}_{\chi,2,1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{\chi,2,2}]\cup[\mathcal{R}_{\chi,4,1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{\chi,4,2}]\cup

\displaystyle[\cup_{k\in[K]}\left([\mathcal{R}_{6,1,k,k}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{6,2,k,k}]\cup[\mathcal{R}_{6,1,k,k-1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{6,2,k,k-1}]\right)\cup\left([\mathcal{R}_{8,1,k,k}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{8,2,k,k}]\cup[\mathcal{R}_{8,1,k,k-1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{8,2,k,k-1}]\right)]

except

\displaystyle D_{4,K}:=[\mathcal{R}_{\chi,2,2}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{\chi,2,1}]\cup[\mathcal{R}_{\chi,4,2}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{\chi,4,1}]\cup

\displaystyle[\cup_{k\in[K]}\left([\mathcal{R}_{6,2,k,k}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{6,1,k,k}]\cup[\mathcal{R}_{6,2,k,k-1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{6,1,k,k-1}]\right)\cup\left([\mathcal{R}_{8,2,k,k}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{8,1,k,k}]\cup[\mathcal{R}_{8,2,k,k-1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{8,1,k,k-1}]\right)];

moreover, there is a unique point in

; and

•

let

\displaystyle c_{5}=\max[[\mathcal{R}_{1,2}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{1,1}]\cup[\cup_{k\geq 1}[\mathcal{R}_{5,2,k,k}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{5,1,k,k}]\cup[\mathcal{R}_{5,2,k-1,k}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{5,1,k-1,k}]]]>0;

\displaystyle c_{6}=\min[[\mathcal{R}_{3,1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{3,2}]\cup[\cup_{k\geq 1}[\mathcal{R}_{7,1,k,k}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{7,2,k,k}]\cup[\mathcal{R}_{7,1,k-1,k}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{7,2,k-1,k}]]]<0;

\displaystyle c_{7}=\min[[\mathcal{R}_{2,2}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{2,1}]\cup[\cup_{k\geq 1}[\mathcal{R}_{6,2,k,k}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{6,1,k,k}]\cup[\mathcal{R}_{6,2,k,k-1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{6,1,k,k-1}]]]>0;

\displaystyle c_{8}=\max[[\mathcal{R}_{1,1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{1,2}]\cup[\cup_{k\geq 1}[\mathcal{R}_{5,1,k,k}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{5,2,k,k}]\cup[\mathcal{R}_{5,1,k-1,k}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{5,2,k,k-1}]]]>0;

then

(5.1)

\displaystyle c_{3,K}<c_{6}<0<c_{5}<c_{2,K};\qquad\forall K\in{\mathbb{N}}.

and

\displaystyle c_{8}<c_{7}.

In the following assumptions, we give explicit conditions on graph parameters to guarantee Assumption
5.1
.

Assumption 5.2
.

Let
i,j
be positive integers satisfying

•

\epsilon i\in(V_{p_{1}-1},V_{p_{1}})
,
\epsilon j\in(V_{p_{2}-1},V_{p_{2}})
and

i_{*}\in[n]
such that

a_{i}^{(\epsilon)}=a_{j}^{(\epsilon)}
, and

, and

and

, then
\tau_{i_{*}}\neq\tau_{j_{*}}

, and
b_{i}^{(\epsilon)}=-
and

\displaystyle\tau_{i_{*}}^{-1}\tau_{j_{*}}<e^{V_{p_{2}}-V_{p_{1}-1}}\mathrm{and}\ \tau_{i*}^{-1}\tau_{j_{*}}<e^{V_{p_{2}-1}-V_{p_{1}}}

a_{i}^{(\epsilon)}=a_{j}^{(\epsilon)}

\displaystyle\tau_{i*}^{-1}\tau_{j_{*}}<e^{V_{p_{2}-1}-V_{p_{1}}}\

Assumption 5.3
.

Suppose all the following conditions hold

(1)

, we have

\displaystyle\max\{u,v\}<e^{V_{p_{2}-1}-V_{p_{1}}}\tau_{j_{2}}^{-1}\tau_{j_{1}}

(2)

For any
(j_{1},p_{1})
,
(j_{2},p_{2})
such that
\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{j_{1},p_{1},<,1}}=1
and

\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{j_{2},p_{2},<,1}}=1
, we have

\displaystyle\max\{u,v\}<e^{V_{p_{2}-1}-V_{p_{1}}}\tau_{j_{2}}^{-1}\tau_{j_{1}}

For any
(j_{1},p_{1})
,

such that
\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{j_{1},p_{1},>,0}}=1
and

\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{j_{2},p_{2},>,0}}=1
, we have

\displaystyle\max\{u,v\}<e^{V_{p_{2}}-V_{p_{1}}}\tau_{j_{2}}^{-1}\tau_{j_{1}}
